The Best Last-Minute Gifts That Don't Feel Last Minute

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This series will help you pick the perfect presents, decor, and activities to make this Christmas the Best Christmas Ever.

We’ve all experienced gift panic—that sinking feeling when you realize you need a present in an hour and you just don’t have one to give. Whether you spent all year chasing the “perfect” gift or simply procrastinated, your time is nearly up. But don’t panic. The clock may have nearly run out, but your options haven’t. Before you resort to re-gifting something from the back of a closet, here’s how to pull off a last-minute gift that is effortless but feels elevated and worthy of the person you’re giving it to.

With the right presentation, even the most last-minute gift can read as thoughtful. Any performer will tell you that the difference between a good show and a bad one comes down to commitment and confidence. The same is true for last-minute gifts: If you act like your gift is thoughtful and intentional, they’ll think it is. The biggest mistake people make is apologizing as they hand over a present—nothing ruins a vibe faster than, “Sorry this is so last-minute.” So give with confidence.

And focus on presentation. A handwritten note that contains a shared memory or personal joke can elevate a present. Use simple but personal wrapping; craft paper and twine, or a reusable gift bag that looks like you planned ahead. Add a small flourish, like a sprig of holly or a fancy bow. Small touches can make it seem like you’ve carefully prepared. What you don’t want are obvious panic gifts that scream, “I grabbed this on the way here.” The goal is effortless, not frantic.

Digital gifts are your friends. You can order something in minutes that arrives instantly online, and with a little thought, will still read as personal and intentional. The trick is choosing a present that speaks to the recipient’s tastes, hobbies, or quirks, and pairing it with a small note to show you remember what they love. From e-books to streaming subscriptions, these gifts hit the sweet spot of thoughtful and last-minute. Here are some suggestion:

When time is running out, gift cards are your secret weapon. They arrive instantly online, don’t require shipping, and with the right presentation, they can feel surprisingly thoughtful. The key is to pick a card that fits the recipient’s interests and add a personal note. The more specific you are, the better. If your giftee is into Italian food, get them an Eataly gift card. If they're a PC gamer, get them a Steam card. If they're into education, a membership to Skillshare is perfect. There's a gift card out there for just about any interest, and all you have to do it google it and email it.

Generic gift cards can be a last-minute lifesaver that can still seem a little personal if you give them the right way.

Pick a theme or design: Many online cards, like Amazon, Etsy, or Starbucks, let you choose designs. The right visual makes the card feel more intentional.

Include a warm, personal message: Even one line can make a digital card feel thoughtful—something like, “I couldn’t pick just one thing for you. Hope this helps you treat yourself.”

Send strategically: Deliver the card by email or text, but consider printing the email or screenshotting it in a small envelope so it feels like a “real” gift.

Sign someone up for a subscription service, write a note explaining you want to keep giving them presents all year, and you're golden. Here are some suggestions:

SnackCrate: This service sends a different snack from around the world every month.

SpiceClub: For the culinary adventurer on your list. A subscription to RawSpiceBar’s Spice Club delivers a curated selection of spices every month.

Mocktail Club: Booze-free fancy drinks delivered once a month? Sign me up.

Thera Box: If you know someone in need of some self-care, this once-a-month box is packed with wellness and relaxation items.

Loot Crate: If your giftee is geeky, consider this monthly surprise box of pop culture treasures.

Cameo is a platform where celebrities, influencers, musicians, and performers create personalized video messages for fans. If someone on your list is a fan of someone on Cameo, you can set up a session, and give a card with a mysterious note like "Keep an eye out for a special message." They'll be delighted with a personal message from wrestling legend Mick Foley or terrified when Chris Hansen appears on their screen; your choice.

I have enough crap in my house, so I love getting experiences instead of things. The challenge with experience gifts is usually timing: Many require reservations or coordination, but there are plenty of options that let your giftee schedule the time, making them perfect last-minute gifts.

Museum membership cards: Many institutions sell digital gift memberships that the recipient can activate whenever they like.

Movie tickets: Your local multiplex probably has a gift certificate you can buy online.

Local attraction passes: Some city attractions offer gift cards or “anytime admission” tickets that don’t expire immediately.

Pair these with a note explaining why you chose the experience, or, if you have that kind of relationship, something like, "I want to take you to the Museum of Modern Art." Just make sure you follow through and actually go.

Sometimes, there’s literally no time to order, wrap, or even think about a gift. If you’re scrambling out the door and need something now, you might be able to fake your way through, even if you're stopping at the liquor store on the way to the Christmas party.

Flowers or a small potted plant: Give a seasonal bouquet or a small indoor plant. Add a handwritten note, even a single line, and it might come off as thoughtful.

Gourmet chocolates or candy: Specialty chocolates from a local shop or grocery store display can feel luxe with a little ribbon or bag.

Wine, craft beer, or non-alcoholic beverage: A bottle with a clean label or interesting packaging immediately elevates your gift. Ask the clerk at the liquor store to help you find something unique.

Lottery tickets: Who wouldn't appreciate a bunch of scratch-off lottery tickets?

Hard cash is the most impersonal gift of all, but the only one you know will be used. This gift really depends on the person getting it, so be aware of whether they're likely to think "Just what I wanted!" or "Wait, I'm not poor." If you give cash, make sure it's crisp bills in a nice card, with a personalized note.

Elon Musk once again promises Tesla’s new autonomous driving is better than humans

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elon Musk has a new promise: Version 15 of Full Self-Driving drives better than a real driver. “Completely new architecture that deserves being called ‘super’ intelligence, as it drives better than human intelligence,” he wrote on X after Tesla started sending the update to a first batch of owners.

That’s great news.

It was great news back in November 2023 too, when he wrote: “Over time, Version 12 (end-to-end neural nets) will far exceed human safety even when unsupervised.” Also in August 2025, when he said: “v14 will be better than human for sure, but I don’t know if it will be 10X. Maybe 2X to 3X. v15 has a shot at 10X.” Back then, he offered no data, and no third-party study to support his claim. It’s the same thing now.

Tesla says that the new FSD v15—which Musk says goes to wide release in December—upgrades the neural network that processes the camera sensors into information for the car to drive. It has also swapped the old lo-fi grayscale schematic 3D view for an AI-generated live street scene that looks like GTA VI-ish. The latter is the only thing that we know is real—and I have to admit it looks pretty neat—but nobody knows if the new driving engine works as well as Musk claims.

Same song, new version number

It may not matter too much. The new v15 is still Level 2 autonomous driving, which means drivers will need to watch the road at all times. While Musk says it drives better than a human, it must be a very dumb human because Tesla’s own support page says the software “does not make the vehicle autonomous” and is meant for use “only with a fully attentive driver.” That matters because, when a person sits ready to grab the wheel, every avoided crash could be the human’s save as much as the machine’s.

Tesla claims that the new FSD is “25x better handling of safety critical cases.” Better than what? Phil Duan, who leads self-driving at Tesla, said on X that “v15 is 25x safer than v14 across the fleet.” Musk answered an X user too: “Some people thought we meant 25% improvement, no we mean 25 times!” But we don’t really know about how much “safer” v14 was either. As electric mobility site Electrek points out, without real published figures or a peer-reviewed study—like this on Waymo reducing its crashes with injured people by 85%—that “25x” claim is hard to substantiate.

In a post, the company explains that its new vision encoder—the part of the network that interprets the information from the car’s video cameras—as the reason for that “25x better handling.” Now shared by FSD, Robotaxis, and Optimus humanoids, the engineering team says the new software improves “the critical intervention rate by 25x.” Tesla says the new encoder was trained on “millions of internet videos.”

According to Tesla, v15 adjusts its speed better in bad weather and school zones, steers clear of deep standing water, and is quicker to spot signs, potholes, small animals, chains and junk on the road. It doesn’t say anything about fake Looney Tunes’ painted tunnels.

It can ease off for speed cameras. And it learns from you, so if you cancel the slowdown at the same speed camera twice, by accelerating, it will stop braking at that point of the road. The company also claims that the car’s interior camera—which checks if you are paying attention to the road or not—now works more often when you wear hats and sunglasses.

Neat!

As part of the update, Tesla Reality View has changed. It now looks very similar to the live situational awareness screens you get in some Chinese cars: a hyperrealistic view of the road and your car as seen from behind at a 45-degree angle. You can see vehicles and people in 3D hyperrealistic renderings. Even the buildings are textured and lit like the real thing. It feels like a high-end car racing game. Musk claims “it’s like GTA.”

It looks neat, but it doesn’t seem to convey information better than the older user interface, which showed a much clearer representation of reality. Seen from a high angle, it was a simplified, very stylized version of the road populated with semi-transparent versions of vehicles and nearby people. In the old version, despite its perception problems, it was easy to get instant situational awareness. In the new v15, the realism obscures the information with too much darkness and glitz.

The interface also features a blue ribbon that traces the route FSD intends to drive, so you can correct it. Chevron arrows float overhead before every turn. And the map now shows several parking spots at your destination. Some Chinese car manufacturers are already displaying this information directly on the windshield, projecting computer-generated wide-screen AR graphics over the real world.

Fortunately, you can switch to a split view or just pick the GPS map view. Musk claims “you can tap any point on the reality revisualizer to make your car go there.” 

Musk says that the new UI uses the same visual features the FSD v15 driving model uses: It’s all AI generated. The AI model renders short bursts of eight frames at 36 frames per second, using just half of one of the accelerators in the car’s AI4 computer.

Still, the video may lag behind reality because the AI is not fast enough. Musk admits the lag is “a bit high due to compute constraints.” He claims Tesla has a way to trim it by about 35%. More promises.

If December’s wide release arrives on time (which may or may not happen), it will put the new FSD in a lot more hands. In 2027 we will be able to test the real value of Musk’s promises once again. Let’s stay positive. Maybe this will be it. You know what they say: 15th time’s the charm.

→ Baca artikel asli

Is Heat Escaping Your House? A RISION Thermal Imaging Camera Can Help

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Drafty windows, cold walls and rising heating bills? The RISION Magic Series helps you spot potential trouble areas before they become expensive problems.

Sponsored By: RISIONThis piece was created in partnership with RISION, your go-to for thermal imaging cameras and smart sensing devices.

Imagine cranking up the thermostat on a freezing winter morning to get cozy, only to find that somehow your living room still feels chilly. Maybe there’s a draft coming from the windows, or one area never seems to warm up. Whatever the reason, you might be heating a home that’s letting the warmth escape—and paying through the nose for it.

According to the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency’s ENERGY STAR program, you can save an average of 15% on heating and cooling costs by sealing air leaks and adding insulation where it’s needed. The tricky part? Figuring out exactly where the problems are.

That’s where a thermal imaging camera comes in handy. Instead of guessing which windows need resealing or where insulation might be missing, you can use one to spot temperature differences and take a closer look.

I recently got my hands on both the RISION Magic and Magic Pro thermal cameras and spent time with them around my house. From scanning windows and exterior walls to checking HVAC vents and even tracking my own footprints across the carpet, I was impressed by how much these tiny cameras could reveal.

Better yet, both models are on sale for up to 45% off during Black Friday sales, making now an excellent time to pick one up.

A thermal imaging camera detects infrared radiation emitted by objects and turns this into a visual representation of surface temperatures. Warmer (red, orange) and cooler (blue, purple) areas appear in contrasting colors, allowing you to see temperature differences that you can’t see with your own eyes.

This opens up some useful possibilities for homeowners. Imagine scanning around a window and spotting a cooler section along the frame. That doesn’t automatically mean there’s an air leak, but it’s a good reason to check the weatherstripping, caulking or surrounding insulation.

You can also use thermal imaging to check on other common household issues:

But remember: thermal imaging isn’t a crystal ball. There can be several reasons for surface temperature differences, and the camera won’t tell you exactly what’s wrong behind a wall. Still, it can help narrow down the places worth checking out before you start ripping off trim or making repairs.

That’s the appeal of the RISION Magic Series. The camera plugs directly into a compatible USB-C smartphone, tablet or computer, so there’s no need to carry around a bulky device. This thing is about the size of a packet of mints.

I spent a couple of days with the RISION Magic and Magic Pro, and one of my favorite things about these cameras is how little effort it takes to get started.

There’s no tiresome setup process or annoying account registration. I took the camera out of its box, plugged it into my phone, downloaded the app and started viewing the thermal feed. The whole process took less than two minutes. Considering how many devices require an email address, password and account verification before you can use them, this was a refreshing change.

The app itself is just as straightforward. You get a live thermal image and simple buttons to capture a photo or record a video. That’s about all there is to it, and that’s all you really need.

I was super impressed by the camera’s sensitivity. As I walked around my house, I could actually see the residual warmth of my footprints on the carpet. Plugged-in electronics appeared as warm spots, while my air-conditioning vents stood out in deep blue against the warmer ceiling.

The app also automatically highlighted particularly hot and cold areas with temperature readouts, making it easy to identify points of interest without constantly examining the entire image. Every now and then, the hotter readouts were difficult to read because the red writing was so close to the red heat source, but a little shift in camera angle normally fixes it.

Editor’s note: Both cameras require a compatible USB-C device and RISION’s accompanying software. They do not support Lightning connectors.

The RISION Magic is a great entry point if you’re looking to investigate insulation problems, cold spots or other temperature-related issues around the house. It’s small enough to slip into a pocket and plugs directly into your phone’s USB-C port, so there’s no separate battery or bulky equipment to worry about.

It features a native 160-by-120-pixel thermal sensor, enhanced to 320-by-240 pixels using RISION’s QuadIR processing technology. There’s also a 25Hz refresh rate, which keeps the image reasonably smooth as you move the camera around.

During testing, I liked how clearly the thermal images showed the framing behind my exterior walls. I could easily make out the positions of wall studs thanks to the subtle temperature differences across the surface. As someone who’s occasionally had trouble getting consistent readings from traditional stud finders, I found this useful, though I’d still confirm stud locations before drilling.

I also scanned around my windows, looking for cold spots that might indicate gaps in the seals or in the surrounding insulation. Thankfully, I didn’t find any major areas of concern. My house is only about two years old, so that was reassuring.

The Magic makes this type of inspection quick and easy, even if you have no previous experience with thermal cameras.

Black Friday deal: Normally $199, the RISION Magic is just $109 (45% off) during the sale. It’s also available on Amazon.

If you’re regularly tackling home renovations, HVAC repairs or more technical troubleshooting, the RISION Magic Pro gives you an extra level of detail.

The main difference is image resolution. The Magic Pro features a native 256-by-192-pixel thermal sensor, enhanced to 512-by-384 pixels through QuadIR processing. This step-up in detail can make it easier to distinguish smaller temperature variations when examining electrical components, HVAC equipment or other mechanical systems.

It’s almost identical to the standard Magic; the only way you can tell them apart is the coloring on the units themself. They both work exactly the same way; the Pro version just offers higher resolution.

For a regular homeowner, the extra resolution could be useful during larger renovation projects or more thorough home inspections. But the real appeal is for contractors, HVAC technicians, appliance repair professionals and experienced DIYers who want more information from their thermal images.

One unexpectedly useful application I found while testing was identifying cooler patches on carpeting. Damp areas can appear noticeably colder than the surrounding floor, which could make the camera handy for investigating spills or even the occasional pet accident. It’s not a substitute for a moisture meter, but it can help you figure out where to look before a spill dries.

Black Friday deal: The Magic Pro drops from $299 to $199 (33% off) during RISION’s Black Friday sale. You can also shop the Magic Pro on Amazon.

Winter is a good time to be checking your home’s insulation and draft problems, particularly when the temperature difference between indoors and outdoors makes cold spots easier to identify. And with Black Friday discounts on both RISION Magic Series cameras, you can pick up a useful tool at a discount.

Through November 30, RISION is offering its lowest prices of the year, with discounts of up to 45% across its product lineup. The RISION Magic is our pick for everyday homeowners, especially at its $109 sale price. Meanwhile, the RISION Magic Pro offers more advanced imaging for those who need it, with a $100 discount bringing its price down to $199.

A thermal camera won’t fix a drafty window or replace missing insulation, but it can help you figure out where to focus your efforts. And when you’re trying to keep your home comfortable without sending your heating bill through the roof, knowing where to start is half the battle.

‘Grand Theft Auto VI’ Album’s Secret Weapon? The ‘Barbie’ Soundtrack Creator

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Atlantic Music Group president Kevin Weaver helped craft the video game's wide and diverse soundtrack, which features the likes of Keith Richards, Morgan Wallen, Lana Del Rey and Travis Scott.

For the biggest game the studio has ever released, Grand Theft Auto VI developer Rockstar Games wanted an equally ambitious soundtrack. Like much of the entertainment business these days, they turned to Atlantic Records to help make that happen.

Over the past decade, the record label’s soundtrack efforts, spearheaded by Atlantic Music Group President Kevin Weaver, have established Atlantic as the de-facto landing spot for media companies looking to build a star-studded accompanying album for their work. Since 2015, Atlantic has handled the soundtracks for movies including Furious 7, Suicide Squad, Greatest Showman, F1, Twisters and most-famously Barbie, the latter of which became a cultural phenomenon alongside the film itself.

But the biggest opportunity Atlantic has seen to this point wasn’t a movie, but rather a video game.

“I went to Rockstar and said that they control the most culturally relevant IP on the planet, and that what they do with music is incredible,” Weaver told The Hollywood Reporter over the phone from Miami — where he’d spent much of last week attending launch events for the game — as he recalled the initial pitch to the company. “Music is such an endemic part of the game and what GTA is. Why don’t we endeavor to do something similar to what I’d done on Barbie and these other soundtracks and A&R a whole lot of new records?”

Weaver produced the soundtrack along with fellow Atlantic execs Joseph Khoury, Cody Verdecias, and Brandon Davis. GTA marked new territory for Weaver, the first time he’d taken on a video game soundtrack, which as he acknowledges, wasn’t on his purview before the game came into the fold.

“I’d never been interested in doing a video game soundtrack, to be honest with you,” Weaver says. “We license a lot of music to games, and I’ve had plenty of game companies come to me over the years, but if I’m not moving the needle and elevating the next thing I do, I’m not doing it. This was a natural choice, this was the one.”

Similar to the process with film soundtracks, early on, Rockstar showed Atlantic some key scenes from the game they wanted music for that were mostly set to temp tracks to help show the overall tone and feel they were looking for.

Among the first artists they reached out to, Weaver says, was Travis Scott, with some of the other earlier asks including Morgan Wallen and Dua Lipa. Scott, Weaver says, turned in the first song, with “Rhyno,” one of the soundtrack’s initial singles. Another Scott track, “Burn This Shit Down,” plays prominently during a key mission in chapter one.

The official soundtrack itself is just one aspect in the wider, rich musical world Rockstar has created for GTA VI. French electronic duo Justice, for example, provided the game’s score. GTA’s musical lifeblood, though, is the game’s radio stations, which feature hundreds of songs across two dozen channels, hosted by real-life celebrities like Robyn, Wallen, Bad Bunny, Keith Richards and Lana Del Rey among many more. Atlantic didn’t handle the radio stations, but Weaver says the company and Rockstar were “attached at the hip,” and some of those soundtrack offers came simultaneously with asks for the stars to host the radio shows.

Rockstar’s vision wasn’t set on any one genre, with the soundtrack spanning country, rock, hip-hop, blues, pop, electronic and latin among other genres. Just days into release, two of the game’s needle drops have gone viral, with the game breathing new life into Carly Rae Jepsen’s cult classic “When the Lights Go Out” and Dire Straits’ “Money For Nothing.” (Neither of those songs is on the official soundtrack, but they play in key missions in the game’s first two chapters.) Jepsen has already re-released her song, hoping to build off the momentum and finally land it on the Hot 100.

“Something Rockstar identified to us right away — this isn’t a rap game,” Weaver says. “It’s not a country game or dance music game. All of this music lives within the game in so many different facets, and that gives us a great opportunity to go out and create records across pretty much every genre we want. It’s GTA’s taste and tone that makes this work.”

The only real through-line, Weaver says, was capturing what he refers to as “the glitz and grime” of Vice City and Leonida, the game’s stand-ins for Miami and the broader Florida area.

While Atlantic’s more than used to handling a rollout for a film soundtrack, the strategy for video games is significantly different. While a movie’s lifespan in the immediate zeitgeist is typically a few weeks — pre-release marketing, the main theatrical window and post-theater streaming — a video game like GTA will hold a captive audience for years. GTA V had a dedicated audience for 13 years before the latest game finally came out last week.

“The tail on this never ends,” Weaver says. “Records are being discovered in the game in real time right now as the game is coming to market as it’s come to market in the last 24 hours, so we kind of have an opportunity to see which records really start to raise their hand as well.”

In the days since the game came out, Rockstar and Atlantic already surprise released a deluxe version of the soundtrack with 23 more songs atop the original 34, and today, they dropped four more songs from 100 gecs, Favored Nations, Joe Armon-Jones and Alex Frankel as iTunes exclusives.

As for any more musical plans going forward, Weaver says “the world’s just gonna have to wait and see.”

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's GTA 6's world, and we're just living in it. Rockstar's latest release is an astonishing technical achievement to behold, confounding even developers working on big-budget games like Naughty Dog's Intergalactic: The Heretic Prophet. It's been 13 long years since GTA 5, but seeing the staggering advancements in visual detail, depth of interactivity, and overall scale in GTA 6, you'd think it's been even longer. And yet, CD Projekt Red says we'll see a similar technical leap in The Witcher 4 compared to The Witcher 3, which came out in 2015.

During the Q&A portion of CDPR's latest earnings call, joint-studio CEO Michał Nowakowski fields a question about GTA 6 and whether The Witcher fans can expect a similar improvement from The Witcher 4. He says the team at CDPR "did look at [GTA 6]" and "did see the differences," and "we believe there's gonna be a comparable leap between Witcher 3 and Witcher 4."

"It's been 11 years, we are working on The Witcher 4 to make it significantly better looking and improved in terms of pretty much every possible facet of the game, so yes, you can expect us to make a much bigger jump between The Witcher 3 and The Witcher 4," says Nowakowski.

There aren't many (read: any) studios with GTA 6's budget, which of course is supplied by Rockstar parent company Take-Two Interactive, but CDPR definitely has the resources to craft huge, visually stunning worlds packed with detail and complex systems that rival today's most expansive AAA games. Cyberpunk 2077 is the most recent example, but even 11 years on, The Witcher 3 absolutely holds up to modern open-world standards.

We're still a couple of years out from The Witcher 4's estimated 2028 release, but there's every reason to believe it'll make The Witcher 3 look downright dated, even after its recent remaster. Whether it'll truly mark a leap comparable to what Rockstar managed from GTA 5 to GTA 6 is a very different question we'll have to wait to answer.

2027 Toyota Sienna Now Gets Up To 37 MPG Combined, And It'll Be Better To Drive Too

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you're still hating on minivans in The Year Of Our Lord 2026, the joke is officially on you. Minivans are exceptionally versatile vehicles that are objectively the best option for most modern SUV buyers, but their lack of a perceived cool factor prevents many buyers from even considering them. We love minivans, and one of the most compelling minivans on the market today is the hybrid-only Toyota Sienna. It's getting even more compelling for 2027, since Toyota has bestowed it with some significant drivetrain improvements including the automaker's sixth-generation hybrid system that uses high efficiency electric motors, a retuned engine, and a more energy efficient lithium-ion battery.

The 2027 Toyota Sienna isn't visibly distinguishable from the 2026 model since no changes were made to its exterior, but minivans have always been about what's beneath the skin, anyway. In addition to the new powertrain, all 2027 Siennas get the larger 12.3-inch infotainment screen and the larger 12.3-inch digital gauge cluster as standard, Toyota's latest suite of active-safety features, an integrated dash cam, a redesigned steering wheel and column, and new LED interior lights.

The Sienna's new hybrid powertrain has been totally recalibrated to operate the 2.5-liter four-cylinder at lower engine speeds when accelerating through the low and mid-range, and all Siennas now have a 1.15-kWh lithium-ion battery and a 166-horsepower electric motor. The powertrain produces a combined 251 horsepower, 6 more horses than last year, and Toyota reckons it's good for a 0.3-second quicker 0-to-60 sprint of 7.5 seconds in front-wheel-drive form. It can still be had with all-wheel drive, which adds a rear-mounted 54-hp electric motor, but its 0-to-60 time is actually a tenth slower than the front-drive model. Toyota now estimates both FWD and AWD Siennas will return 39 mpg in the city, 35 mpg on the highway, and 37 mpg combined, improvements of 3 mpg for FWD and 4 mpg for AWD.

Toyota says the new steering system is more direct and has better on-center feel while reducing steering effort at low speeds for easier parking thanks to a new steering gear box, motor control unit, and torque sensor. The Sienna's brakes have also been reworked with a new electronic control system, offering improved feel and response with less body roll. The latest Toyota Safety Sense 4.0 suite of active safety features has upgraded sensors and hardware for improved obstacle detection capabilities, and in case that all fails, a built-in dash cam comes standard.

Other little changes include the cancellation of the Limited model, now leaving base LE, XLE, Woodland, XSE, and top Platinum trim levels. Toyota has not yet release pricing for the updated 2027 Sienna, but we expect its prices to increase slightly from their current starting point of $42,915.

GTA 6 has a smut book club and a surprisingly long reading list

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Stealing cars, robbing banks, and getting into shootouts are all familiar ways to spend an afternoon in Grand Theft Auto VI. Apparently, there's also time for a little bit of reading.

Rockstar stocked its fictional Florida with books, magazines, and comics that players can pick up and flip through. There's a fishing magazine, little libraries across Leonida with their own reading lists, advertisements for "romantasy" novels on the radio, and even a backyard book club. Here's where to find something to read, what flipping through the pages can unlock, and how to drop in on that aforementioned book club. (I know I want to.)

GTA 6 has 34 reading collectibles, according to Power Pyx. The game groups 27 under Magazines, Books & Comics and tracks another seven at roadside libraries called Book Swaps. There's war fiction, crime memoir, self-help, sci-fi, and adventure, along with comics and magazines about everything from bodybuilding to fishing.

The books in GTA 6 range from crime memoirs to war novels and self-help parodies, and players can read a short excerpt from each.

At Jason's house in Key Lento, Lethal by Design opens to six pages from Chapter 7, where a small-time crook recounts working for Liberty City’s Ancelotti crime family.

By Jason's bed, the World War II novel Ashes of Glory offers six pages in which Captain Liberty and his squad of convicts survive a mission and realize no one planned for them to get out alive.

There's even a military self-help parody called ORCA: A Whole Different Animal, in which a veteran turns his training into life advice and brags about waking up at 2 a.m. to carry a 350-pound log to work.

An issue of Body Copy also gives players a magazine to flip through, from a fitness influencer's morning routine to gym etiquette and places to meditate outdoors.

The game tucks reading material into everyday stops, too. There's a copy of self-help book Help Me Help You Help Me at the Starlet Motel's front desk, and the comics Ratface and Special Friends Force share a rack at a food mart in Vice City's La Perle neighborhood. A trailer in Port Gellhorn has something a little harder to classify, though Dicked Down Deep in Space certainly sounds like sci-fi.

Some of Leonida's books can be spotted from the road. The game features seven roadside libraries called Book Swaps, each holding a few pages from a different adventure novel. For example, down the street from Jason's house, a turtle-shaped bookcase on Dolphin Drive holds Killer Prom. On Vespucci Island, a miniature lighthouse contains Goodnight, Dollface, Goodnight. You'll also find a boat-shaped shelf by Lake Leonida in Ambrosia with Humanity vs Space and a coffin-shaped bookcase outside Capri's Halloween House with Badlands Revenge inside.

The collection doesn't stop there. Three more Book Swaps are scattered across Leonida, including The Wizard's Ruin: Loud Clap of the Night in Watson Bay, Thighlander: A Tale of True Scotsmen in Southside, and Outback Gas Junkie in Waning Sands. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Once a book or magazine catches your eye, look for the Examine prompt. On PlayStation, R1 turns the page, L1 goes back, and Triangle displays the text. Square flips the publication over to show the back cover.

To log a Book Swap, take the book out and put it back. It counts whether you flip through the pages or immediately get back in the car.

Reading in GTA 6 can be a fun detour that leads to new locations and side quests.

At Jason's house, pick up the fishing magazine Off the Hook from the workbench in the shed. Turn to the fishing guide, and five spots appear on your map, including a stretch of water east of Key Lento where you can catch barracuda and a spot beneath the Draper Island bridge where you can fish for tarpon.

Reading can also prompt to a phone call. Outside Three Sheets Liquor, there's a flyer from a man named Kirk who's looking for a creature called the Skunk Ape. Read it to add Kirk to your in-game contacts, then call him at night to start a side quest that takes you looking for the creature near Pelican River.

The phone comes in handy with style magazines, too. Scanning them with the WAiNK app can unlock hairstyles, makeup, and tattoos, which count toward the game’s separate Discovered Styles checklist. And back at Jason's house, Insider Gaming found that flipping through books and magazines gives his Focus meter a boost. Focus lets players slow down time in combat, so a little reading can come in handy the next time Jason gets into a shootout.

Reading also counts toward 100 percent completion, according to Power Pyx. Players need any 30 of the 34 unique titles across Magazines, Books & Comics and Book Swaps. Both collections are under Progress → Collections → Exploration.

Some copies become inaccessible as the story moves on, so Chapter 1 is a good time to look around Jason's place.

And if you prefer reading in a group setting, don't worry. There's even a book club in GTA 6. In the Chapter 3 side mission "Climbing the Cellerichain," Lucia drops off a package for her friend Andy at a house in Waning Sands. When she knocks, someone calls her around to the backyard, where a group of women are discussing a seemingly steamy novel called Two Pricks in the Bush. Lucia can leave once she drops off the package, or she can stick around and give her opinion on the book.

TikTok user @scorpionlibrarian shared a clip of the encounter with the caption “They ended up kicking me out." One commenter asked, "Where can I sign up."

There's clearly a market for these "romantasy" novels in Vice City: During his playthrough, Mashable's tech reporter Alex Perry heard an ad for a smut novel on the in-game radio.

Anthropic lets MCP servers ship skills and run background jobs in Claude

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Shopify, GitHub and Supabase are among the first to use the new features in Claude and Claude Code.

Anthropic has opened Skills over MCP in public beta, letting apps that connect to Claude ship playbooks alongside their tools. Tasks, for long background jobs, and Elicitation, which pauses for user input, are now generally available, with File Uploads coming soon.

Apps that plug into Claude through MCP can now also tell it how to use them, Anthropic said on Tuesday. The feature, Skills over MCP, is in public beta in Claude and Claude Code.

MCP is the open standard Anthropic launched two years ago to link AI agents to outside apps and data. Servers built on it already list the tools Claude can use. With skills, they can also ship step-by-step guides and best practices, which Claude loads only when needed.

Shopify uses skills to help Claude design and update online stores, and HubSpot to onboard customers. Skills update with the server, so users have nothing extra to install. More apps are opening up, too: Canva opened its MCP server to all developers on Tuesday.

The beta is on by default for Pro, Max and Ultra plans. Team admins can switch it on, and Enterprise support is coming soon.

Two other features are now generally available. Tasks lets a server run long jobs in the background while Claude keeps working, which ZoomInfo uses for deeper research. Elicitation lets a server pause and ask the user for input.

GitHub, for example, makes users type a repository’s name before an agent deletes it. Supabase confirms costs before creating a paid project. A fourth feature, File Uploads, will soon let Claude send PDFs, spreadsheets and images to servers.

The features are part of MCP, so servers that support them also work in other compatible apps. Anthropic says MCP now sees over 700M SDK downloads a month. In August, OpenAI and four rivals agreed on a standard for packaging skills and MCP servers.

Skills carry risks, too. That same month, researchers found malicious agent skills that stole passwords and keys after about 1.7 million installs.

Strength-Train Your Hair

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Can the slew of products designed to fortify weakened strands really made a difference? Dermatologists and beauty scientists detail what to look for when you’re shopping the haircare aisle

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

WE’D ALL LIKE to imagine our hair as strong, resilient, healthy. And there’s no shortage of products marketed toward reaching that end point. You’ve got your bond-building treatments, your protein masks, your strengthening serums—heck, there are now entire product lines geared toward promoting robust, rugged strands.

But what exactly does it mean to have ‘strong’ hair?

We tend to equate strength with the kind of glossy, thick, bouncy strands you'd see in a shampoo commercial. But there’s more to the science than that. For starters, hair that looks healthy isn't necessarily strong, and hair that's naturally fine or thinning isn't automatically weak. Plus, there's a difference between making hair “feel” stronger and actually improving its ability to withstand breakage.

Meet the experts: Sherry Maragh, MD, is a board-certified dermatologist based in Virginia. Nathan Puksta is the founder and CEO of FILAMENT Sciences, a haircare brand focused on the science of stronger hair.

So, what counts as strong hair, and can all these products really deliver on their promises? We asked two experts to explain.

Scientifically speaking, hair strength is all about resilience, meaning how well an individual strand withstands stress from brushing, stretching, styling, and other everyday activities without breaking. That strength largely comes from two parts of the hair shaft: the cuticle, which acts as its protective outer layer, and the cortex, which contains keratin proteins responsible for much of its structural integrity, explains Sherry Maragh, MD, a board-certified dermatologist based in Virginia.

Another important factor is elasticity, or the ability of hair to stretch and return toward its original shape without snapping. One of the most useful measurements is tensile strength, which quantifies how much force a strand can withstand before breaking, says Nathan Puksta, the founder and CEO of FILAMENT Sciences, which creates products and ingredients dedicated to the science of stronger hair. Think of it as the difference between a brittle piece of dry spaghetti and a flexible rubber band.

Tensile strength is an important part of this conversation because it’s something that laboratories can actually measure, using calibrated equipment that pulls individual hair fibers until they break. Other tests evaluate elasticity, breakage, and resistance to combing. But strength isn't synonymous with thickness, density, or shine. "Hair can absolutely be strong while still being fine, thin, or less reflective," says Dr. Maragh. "Similarly, hair that appears thick and glossy is not necessarily resistant to breakage." In other words, those naturally fine strands might be remarkably resilient, while locks that epitomize the “glass hair” trend could be surprisingly fragile.

Unfortunately, the things we regularly do to make our hair look good can also compromise its strength. Puksta identifies five major contributors to weakened hair: chemical processing, mechanical damage, heat, environmental exposure, and aging.

Chemical treatments, particularly bleaching and certain straightening processes, can be especially damaging because they alter the hair's internal structure. But everyday habits matter, too. Those can look like:

So, can you actually make your hair stronger? Yes, but there's an important distinction between improving damaged hair's performance and returning it to its original, undamaged condition. "The visible hair shaft is made up of nonliving cells, so damaged hair cannot biologically heal in the way skin does," says Dr. Maragh.

That doesn't mean strengthening products are useless. Certain ingredients can improve hair's physical properties, reduce friction, reinforce weakened areas, and help prevent additional breakage, with some technologies even working beneath the cuticle. Puksta explains that bond-building treatments target chemical interactions within hair, while certain peptides and proteins can help reinforce its keratin structure. However, effectiveness varies considerably between formulations.

Dr. Maragh cautions that an ingredient's ability to penetrate the hair shaft doesn't automatically prove that it repairs structural damage. So, what can different strengthening products realistically accomplish?

And remember that a product claiming 80 percent stronger hair isn't necessarily saying what you think. Dr. Maragh recommends examining what was measured, the comparison group, and whether results came from laboratory testing or consumer feedback. Protein retention, for example, isn't the same measurement as tensile strength.

A 2026 Women’s Beauty Award winner, this wash is prized for its ability to deeply hydrate and help detangle, cutting down on pulling while brushing. It also contains ingredients that battle oxidative stress on the scalp, helping follicles hang onto hair for longer.

A solid conditioner that helps detangle and cut down on styling damage is worth its weight in gold. Dove’s product contains a peptide complex that helps to repair damage.

Oribe Hair Alchemy Fortifying Treatment Serum

This serum contains a curative complex of chia seed, plant protein, and bio-fermented bamboo leaf that the brand claims can penetrate the cortex to heal from within. But perhaps its biggest plus is that it can protect hair from heat damage up to 450 degrees.

FILAMENT TS.02/Tt: Tensile Strength Hair Treatment Mask

Featuring a patented complex that improves hairs’ tensile strength, this mask also contains a plant oil blend for moisturization and protection.

In a small study conducted by the brand, this biotin-free bioactive blend was shown to support hair density and thickness.

Here's the good news: You don't need naturally thick, glossy hair to have strong hair. And if your strands are damaged, there are meaningful ways to improve their resilience. The key is understanding what products can—and cannot—accomplish. A conditioner that reduces breakage through easier detangling is valuable, even if it doesn't rebuild internal bonds. Likewise, a treatment that improves measurable tensile strength isn't necessarily reversing every aspect of previous damage (translation: it can’t recreate virgin hair).

Ultimately, maintaining strength is as much about preventing damage as treating it. Consistent conditioning, gentler styling practices, and choosing products suited to your hair type can help protect existing strands while healthy new hair grows.

And that's worth remembering the next time you see a bottle that promises stronger hair: The goal is helping the strands withstand whatever you put them through—not just have shampoo commercial-worthy hair.

‘Clashing Through The Snow’ Director Carlson Young Breaks Down Buc-ee’s Knockoff, Ryan’s Counsel From Chat GPT & Her Way Into The “Holiday Hijinks” Rom-Com

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Clashing Through the Snow director Carlson Young found it easy to relate to the film’s two main characters, Ryan Mitchell (Christopher Briney) and Hannah Hendricks (Michelle Randolph).

The story centers the pair, two senior editors competing to oust the other from a condensed role due to impending layoffs, chasing big book deals for the publishing company where they work. Ryan operates off of commercial motivation and tapping into what is popular to sell books while Hannah identifies as more of a literary purist who takes big swings with the depth of books she takes on.

“[The script] really spoke to me. I felt so connected to Hannah and Ryan’s literary perspectives, and it felt like a existential conversation that I have with myself, moment to moment, constantly,” Young told Deadline. “It was easy for me to access their personalities and their inner worlds. And then I just loved the classic, holiday hijinks that ensue from there, and I was up for the challenge of trying to make a great holiday rom-com.” Watch on Deadline

The director connected the differing stances on what sells to challenges she navigates in her own job “balancing art and commerce, and what is and isn’t viable in storytelling and in the marketplace.”

RELATED: 7 New Christmas Movies Coming Out In 2026 From ‘Ebenezer’ To ‘Clashing Through The Snow’

“It is true that publishing and cinemas, these are tectonically shifting industries, and trying to find yourself your voice either as a book editor or director, whatever you’re doing, it doesn’t even need to be in a creative field,” she added. “But we’re all existing in these shifting sands, and so that was my way in. That’s why I was looking at it like that.”

In the below interview, Young unpacks specific moments like Lukas Gage’s “scene stealer” appearance, the Buc-ee’s knockoff Luc-ee’s, Ryan’s seeking advice by talking to ChatGPT and more.

DEADLINE: I spoke with Chris and Michelle earlier, and they mentioned you gave Chris a note comparing him to Jim Carrey. Did you have any other references? Could you expand on that?

CARLSON YOUNG: When we were rehearsing, I kept looking at him and seeing this physical comedy coming to the surface, and I was like, “This is like some Jim Carrey. I love this for you. Go off on that.” We tweaked it and trimmed it and shaped it in the edit, but he’s really funny. I hope that he does a lot more comedy.

DEADLINE: What was your role in the Luc-ee’s knockoff to Buc-ee’s?

YOUNG: I don’t know what I can and can’t say about Buc-ee’s/ Luc-ee’s, but we did our best. We did our version. I’m from Texas, Buc-ee’s is a big part of the psychology of a Texan, and so it was really funny, and we did our own version of it.

DEADLINE: For the part where Hannah’s in the helicopter venting to the pilot and Ryan’s driving the truck talking to AI, was there ever a plan to have him talking to a person or was it always going to be ChatGPT?

YOUNG: I have to say, yes, there was [a different iteration]. I personally thought that him talking to Chat was so Ryan. It made me laugh so much. And the amount of times that I’ve caught my husband… I’m like, ‘Are you asking ChatGPT a question? Like for real? Are you serious? ‘And he is serious. It just felt really modern and so of the times, and something like his character would actually do. And I like a lot the responses that Chat has for him. It just made me laugh.

DEADLINE: Lukas Gage is so funny in the small role he plays. He had a similar comedic presence in People We Meet on Vacation. How did you want to incorporate him in the storyline?

YOUNG: He’s a scene-stealer. You just give him an inch, and he will create a million miles of gold. He’s a spinster. And when we Facetimed about the character, he had the funniest ideas and instincts on it, like ‘He’s like Chris Elliott in Scary Movie.” I absolutely loved that. “That’s our north star. Let’s go do that.” He’s such a breath of fresh air once they get to the Mercyville Inn, and he’s such a phenomenal human. We’ll see him in, like, 100 more movies.

DEADLINE: Where did you shoot? Ryan and Hannah come from LA, but they get to New York on a road trip. How did you balance the different locations?

YOUNG: We shot in New York, a bit in the city, a bit upstate. We were shooting the Hudson Valley as you know Indiana and Colorado and Utah and it definitely lent itself. We were also experiencing historic winter weather in New York, like blizzards and production shut down. Many a hurdle, and so it was truly ‘Clashing Through the Snow.’ We had a big snow budget, and we didn’t have to use it because it was just snow everywhere.

DEADLINE: Did the snow budget get redirected to other things?

YOUNG: Absolutely, girl. Our producers would have something to say about that.

DEADLINE: My mind goes to the music, which Prime Video is always good at getting, but the Olivia Rodrigo songs specifically…

YOUNG: That’s how we got the Olivia Rodrigo song. I like that story. I think we should go with that. We saved on snow, so we got this incredible Olivia Rodrigo song.

DEADLINE: Not only “Begged” in the film but “Take Me Home (4 Christmas)” was in the trailer.

YOUNG: I love her song in the film as well. We’re super lucky to have her.

DEADLINE: You’ve done Upgraded with Prime Video as well as The Last Sunrise. I know you’re not doing Upgraded 2, but is this a partnership you’re willing to continue, maybe in the form of The Last Sunrise sequel film when those books come out?

YOUNG: Absolutely, I could, definitely, and I just generally love working with this team at Amazon. They’re so collaborative and really cool people who have their finger on the pulse and they know what’s up. I feel like it’s such a creative environment that I love to work inside of. So yeah, ‘til the day I die, absolutely.

Starboard Expands Into Freight Services With Launch of New Network

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

TORONTO, Nov. 24, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Starboard has launched the Starboard Network, which introduces additional freight services for forwarders operating in Toronto and Montreal.

The network provides freight forwarders with access to LCL import and export rates, drayage across Canada, and airport recovery and delivery in Toronto.

Starboard has partnered with over 25 trucking companies for the drayage service that the company offers. The airport recovery service in Toronto from Starboard also provides a 12-hour removal guarantee. The LCL rates are available through the company's co-loading network.

Until now, Starboard was mostly involved in assisting freight forwarders with the process of finding rates and generating quotes. The new Starboard Network adds an important extra layer.

Rather than managing numerous provider relationships, forwarders can use Starboard as their main point of contact for bookings, operations, billing, and collections.

"We are excited to partner with Starboard to launch a unique offering for Toronto customers," said Pulkit Malhotra of Core Logistics. "Combining Starboard's technology with our physical infrastructure and trucking assets means customers get best-in-class service, reliability and competitive prices."

Starboard plans to launch the network in New York City in December. More services and locations will be added in the coming months.

The Starboard Network is currently available to Starboard clients in Toronto and Montreal.

Starboard provides AI-powered freight rate sourcing and quoting software for freight forwarders. Its platform connects with airlines, ocean carriers, and other freight providers to help teams find rates and respond to quote requests faster.

For more information, visit www.starboard.biz.

Chasing the Camino Coast

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With nearly 14 miles to hike today, we started early at 6:30 AM.

Because Vigo is such a massive port city, our morning walk began right along the waterfront. We passed a mile-long fish distribution facility where workers offloaded fresh seafood from ships into warehouses before loading it onto smaller delivery vans.

Standing guard near the loading zone was a man carrying a live falcon. He explained that his job is to scare away seagulls that constantly try to swoop in and steal the fish.

From here, we officially transitioned onto a mix of the Coastal Camino and the parallel Senda Litoral (Litoral Route). While both follow the ocean, the Litoral Route hugs the shoreline much more closely—even winding all the way out around various peninsulas.

After two miles of walking beside roaring city traffic, the trail finally turned into a quiet, intimate medieval village. We took advantage of the peaceful atmosphere and stopped at a local café for pastries and caffeine.

Leaving the café, we wound through narrow village streets and stepped onto a coastal boardwalk stretching over a marshy area beside the bay. We were officially on the Litoral Route now. Because Vigo is a popular starting point for pilgrims walking the minimum 100 km needed to earn their Compostela certificate, we expected to see far fewer crowds on this quieter coastal stretch.

However, the trail wasn’t very well marked, so we had to rely heavily on digital maps to stay on track. Continuing along the waterfront, we passed through a long, tree-lined park dotted with beachfront bars and nightclubs overlooking the sand—an area that must come alive after dark. Even early in the morning, green parakeets were already out grazing in the grass.

The Camino continued right along the water. A cool breeze blew off the ocean, though the bright sun kept us warm as we passed steep, rocky cliffs broken up by small pockets of sandy beaches. By 11:00 AM, we had already covered 9 miles.

We continued bouncing back and forth between the Coastal and Litoral paths. Eventually, the route ran alongside a busy road at the end of a large bay before leading us down a beachside road past an expansive stretch of sand. Crossing over a river, we finally arrived in the village of A Ramallosa, where our albergue was waiting.

Our accommodations for the night were in a family-run apartment building—the family lived on the top two floors and rented out the bottom two. Conveniently, it sat right behind a beachfront bar where we grabbed dinner. We sat outside enjoying the ocean views and a few drinks until the evening chill pushed us indoors, where we exhaustedly turned in for a great night’s sleep.

New York Just Made It Harder for Automakers to Put Car Features Behind a Paywall

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new law takes aim at subscription fees for features already built into your vehicle, but automakers can still charge for certain services.

These days, the average new car payment in America is over $800 a month. With a payment like that, the last thing you want is to pay even more to access all of your car’s features. The state of New York is now taking steps to ensure that won’t happen; however, like most things in life, there are exceptions.

On Monday, November 23, New York governor Kathy Hochul signed legislation restricting automakers and dealers from charging consumers subscription fees to access certain features already installed in their vehicles. Essentially, if the hardware necessary to operate a feature is already installed in the vehicle, and activating that feature doesn't require continued support or expense on the part of the automaker, dealer, or third-party service provider, it cannot be put behind a subscription paywall.

A few years ago, BMW faced backlash in Europe for charging a monthly fee in order to enjoy heated seats. BMW has since backed away from that plan, and under this new legislation, that subscription approach would no longer be legal. According to a report by the New York Post, companies that violate this new law will face a $250 fine per subscription.

However, this doesn’t mean that New Yorkers can wave goodbye to all automotive-related subscription services; automakers can still charge for navigation updates, satellite radio, infotainment services, in-car Wi-Fi, roadside assistance, and certain connected vehicle services. Also, software-dependent driver-assistance and automated driving features like Tesla’s Full Self-Driving and General Motors’s Super Cruise are also exempt, meaning motorists shouldn't expect their hands-free highway driving subscriptions to suddenly become cost-free too.

Automakers and third-party companies have about 90 days to get into compliance before the law takes effect on February 27, 2027. It will be interesting to see if other states will follow New York’s lead, but the bigger question is if automakers will simply begin charging more for optional equipment at the time of purchase.

New Option Approved for Gastrointestinal Stromal Tumors

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

— Adding bezuclastinib to sunitinib improved median PFS by 7 months

The FDA approved the selective KIT inhibitor bezuclastinib (Ekitna), in combination with sunitinib (Sutent), for previously treated gastrointestinal stromal tumors (GIST).

The approval allows use of the combination in adults with advanced GIST previously treated with imatinib.

"With today's FDA approval of bezuclastinib in combination with sunitinib, we have a new standard-of-care treatment following imatinib for patients with GIST," said Andrew Wagner, MD, PhD, of Dana-Farber Cancer Institute in Boston, in an announcement from bezuclastinib-developer Cogent Biosciences. "This approval represents a major step forward for the GIST community and reflects the tireless progress made in improving the lives of people living with this disease."

Sara Rothschild, executive director of the GIST advocacy organization The Life Raft Group, called the approval "an incredibly meaningful milestone for the GIST community that has waited many years hoping, advocating, and helping advance the research for a new treatment option."

Principal support for the approval came from the randomized, international PEAK trial involving 413 patients with locally advanced, unresectable, or metastatic GIST and disease progression after or intolerance to imatinib. Patients were randomized to sunitinib alone or in combination with bezuclastinib, and the primary endpoint was progression-free survival (PFS) by independent review. Crossover to the combination was allowed after confirmed progression with single-agent sunitinib.

The primary analysis showed a median PFS of 16.5 months with the combination versus 9.2 months with sunitinib monotherapy. The difference translated into a 50% reduction in the PFS hazard (HR 0.50, 95% CI 0.39-0.65, P<0.0001). Significantly more patients randomized to the combination achieved objective responses (46% vs 26%, P<0.0001). Overall survival data remained immature.

Adverse reactions to the combination occurring in ≥20% of patients consisted of diarrhea, increased liver enzymes, hypertension, dysgeusia, hair color changes, fatigue, abdominal pain, nausea and vomiting, decreased appetite, stomatitis, gastroesophageal reflux disease, rash, dyspepsia, and pyrexia. The most common (≥5%) grade 3/4 laboratory abnormalities were increased alanine aminotransferase and decreased leukocytes, neutrophils, lymphocytes, and hemoglobin.

The approval brings to six the number of tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKI) for GIST. Imatinib remains the only TKI approved for first-line treatment. Sunitinib alone and the bezuclastinib-sunitinib combination are the only approved second-line therapies. Regorafenib (Stivarga) is approved for use in third line and ripretinib (Qinlock) for fourth line and beyond. Additionally, avapritinib (Ayvakit) has approval for unresectable/metastatic GIST harboring a PDGFRA exon 18 mutation.

Bezuclastinib also is being investigated as a potential treatment for systemic mastocytosis.

Moretto rivela: “Rinnovo di Tomori fermo, possibile cessione a gennaio. Krosche già operativo a Milano”

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nel canale YouTube di Fabrizio Romano, l'esperto di mercato Matteo Moretto ha rivelato informazioni importanti riguardo il calciomercato rossonero. Di seguito le sue parole.

"Su Tomori c'è un prima e un dopo: secondo me qualora Massimiliano Allegri fosse rimasto sulla panchina del Milan, magari in quel caso ci potevano essere delle chance di rinnovo per Tomori. Poi dopo c'è stata la gestione della situazione Tomori da parte del Milan un po' particolare. Tomori è un calciatore che comunque è sempre stato assolutamente professionale e ama profondamente la maglia del Milan, ha sempre dimostrato tutto il suo impegno. È vero, è stato messo ai margini, il Milan ha cercato di trovare delle soluzioni in uscita proprio negli ultimi giorni di mercato. Ha un contratto in scadenza nel 2027. C'erano dei dialoghi in corso per il rinnovo quando c'era Allegri in panchina, poi con il tempo e con il susseguirsi di determinati avvenimenti questo tipo di rinnovo non è più stato discusso. Ad oggi le trattative sono assolutamente ferme. Non c'è dialogo tra le parti e ribadisco, Tomori è in scadenza 2027. Vedremo se a gennaio ci potrà essere una via d'uscita magari con il pagamento di un indennizzo in favore del Milan, oppure se si arriverà a zero a giugno, però insomma ad oggi non c'è non c'è l'ombra di un possibile rinnovo".

"Marcus Krosche è già a Milano, già molto operativo, nonostante non sia ancora ufficiale, ma l'ufficialità è attesa a breve. Si sta lavorando fortemente sul rinnovo di Rabiot, l'intenzione di Cardinale è quella di arrivare alla chiusura del rinnovo entro la fine dell'anno, perché Rabiot è un calciatore imprescindibile per Ruben Amorim. Poi vedremo come si svilupperà il mercato. Il Milan vuole rinforzarsi davanti, Endrick è un nome, insomma sono discorsi che stiamo già raccontando da diverso tempo, poi non appena ci saranno delle novità sostanziali, in un senso o nell'altro ve lo ve lo diremo".

Nigerian monarch, 92, suspended after sexual assault claims

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Umar Farouk Umar has been on the throne since 2007, succeeding his brother, who reigned for 41 years

One of Nigeria's best-known monarchs, the Emir of Daura, has been suspended from all official engagements and travel following allegations of sexual assault by a Polish tourist.

The visitor said Umar Farouk Umar, 92, had grabbed her chest during a tourism encounter and that she felt disgusted by the experience.

The allegation sparked widespread discussion across social media platforms, with many calling for the suspension of the emir and an investigation - calls the Katsina state government has now heeded.

Umar has not commented on the allegations or his suspension.

He is one of several emirs - a traditional role dating back to pre-colonial times - who still wield great cultural and religious influence in northern Nigeria, although they have lost their political power.

Daura, where Umar serves as emir, holds an extremely important position for the Hausa people who dominate northern Nigeria. It is widely regarded as their spiritual homeland and the origin of the Hausa royal lineage, serving as the core of Hausa origin mythology and the legitimate foundation of traditional authority.

Politicians routinely visit his palace for royal prayers and to gain his support.

On her Instagram page, the tourist said she had spent 30 days travelling around Nigeria before visiting the Emir's palace on 14 November.

She posted a video, which has since been deleted, in which a man who appears to be the emir is holding her hands in his lap.

After some moments she was heard saying: "Stop, stop" as the hands reached for her chest.

According to a statement, Katsina Governor Dikko Umaru Radda directed the immediate constitution of a committee to investigate the allegations.

The statement said the state government was taking the allegations seriously and would rely on the committee's findings to determine the appropriate course of action.

It said the committee's findings and recommendations would be fully implemented, stressing that justice would be served.

However, it did not disclose the membership of the investigative committee, its terms of reference or the timeline for submitting its report.

The emir has been on the throne since 2007, succeeding his brother Bashar dan Umaru, who reigned for 41 years.

Living Computers closed, but its spirit and vintage machines live on at a museum south of Seattle

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Kurt Schlosser on Nov 24, 2026 at 11:08 amNovember 24, 2026 at 11:09 am

When Seattle’s Living Computers: Museum + Labs closed for good in 2024, it seemed like the end of the line for anyone who wanted to do more than just look at old computers.

But in a Tukwila business park 20 minutes south of downtown, a sizable collection of vintage computers is still alive — kept running by a few of the same people who once did the same for Paul Allen.

The Interim Computer Museum is a nonprofit that opened to the public in 2024 and has quietly grown into a hands-on showcase of dozens of machines, from early Apples and IBM PCs to hulking DEC systems that once filled university labs. At 4,400 square feet of public space, it’s nearly as big as Living Computers was before that museum expanded in 2016.

ICM is led by Stephen Jones, who calls himself the museum’s “caretaker.” Jones spent two decades helping track down and restore machines for Allen, first at PDP Planet, Allen’s private collection of vintage systems, then at Living Computers, where he was senior engineering manager.

When the late Microsoft co-founder’s estate wound down Living Computers, the majority of the collection went to the Mimms Museum of Technology and Art in Georgia, where Jones is also a curator. The machines in Tukwila are privately owned, and Jones has helped bankroll ICM with personal funds, with plans to eventually donate the collection to the nonprofit.

He chose not to reuse the Living Computers name, which ICM now holds, because “it has a finite past.” Instead, he wants to carry on “the spirit of the engineering focus.”

“Between Mimms and here, this is the legacy,” Jones said. “We show the name and the connection to the past, but we’re doing new things.”

ICM occupies space in Seattle Exchange @ Southcenter, a sprawl of low-slung flex buildings off Strander Boulevard near Westfield Southcenter mall. Its neighbors include a car detailer, a pest control company, a welding shop, an embroiderer and an upholsterer.

More than four decades ago, Seattle Computer Products operated just down Industry Drive. That’s where Tim Paterson wrote 86-DOS, the operating system Microsoft bought and turned into MS-DOS for IBM’s first PC.

Jones calls that proximity part of the “magic” of the location. The museum has a Gazelle computer that SCP built nearby, using boards that Jones said the company hired high school students to assemble and test. Volunteers recently pulled data off its hard drive, which boots into MS-DOS 2.11 and still holds SCP’s own source code.

Allen’s fingerprints are everywhere. One display holds a 1971 letter from Hellmut Golde, director of the University of Washington’s Computer Science Laboratory, kicking Allen and his “friends from the Lakeside School” out of the lab. Among the offenses: tying up the teletypes to print “endless listings,” and taking an acoustic coupler from a professor’s office “without leaving at least a note.” Golde asked them to turn in their keys.

The letter sits beside an Imlac PDS-1, the same type of graphics computer Golde scolded the teens for using without permission. More than five decades later, the letter is headed back to campus: ICM plans to display it, along with the Imlac, at UW’s 60th anniversary celebration for its computer science program in March.

Jones recalled that Allen insisted visitors to Living Computers get “the 10-minute experience” of loading an Altair 8800 from paper tape, just as he remembered it. The kit computer was the machine for which Allen and Bill Gates wrote Microsoft’s first product, and Allen wanted to reinforce “how far we’ve come and how difficult old computers were.” ICM has an Altair 8800 on display.

Around the corner is Miss Piggy, a DEC datasystem 570 that Microsoft used in the early 1980s to support the group that built Multiplan, Word and later Excel. It was also the email host for much of the company, including Gates, who once had to print out an important message on its console printer when the system went down before a speaking engagement.

Recovered email shows Allen pushing to get Piggy networked. “Paul Allen is very keen on doing this,” Henry Burgess wrote in 1982. Retired in 1986, the machine now runs again in Tukwila. It’s one of ICM’s most popular remote systems, and some fans come in just to write programs while sitting next to it.

Other local ties include a PDP-7 that Boeing used to process flight data in the 1960s, and a Toad-1, a modern take on the PDP-10 built in the 1990s by Redmond’s XKL. Jones first got involved with Allen’s world through a 1997 Usenet post inviting PDP-10 enthusiasts onto a Toad. That machine became the cornerstone of PDP Planet.

In a 2024 video for Christie’s, Jones said what inspired Allen was “being able to recover old programs, mythical code, and get them running on hardware so that they can be studied.”

Asked what Allen would make of ICM, Jones didn’t hesitate. “I think he’d be happy,” he said.

On a monitor beside ICM’s PDP-6, a 1968 artificial intelligence program is having trouble.

“SORRY, I DON’T KNOW THE WORD ‘STEEPLE,'” it replies.

The program is SHRDLU, created by Terry Winograd at the MIT AI Lab. It let a user type plain-English commands to move blocks around a virtual world. Only the PDP-6’s front console and display survived, so ICM is rebuilding the machine as a “hybrid,” with the original hardware wired to an emulator.

Jones sees a lesson in that old error message. “You look at this and you think of how we use ChatGPT now,” he said. “The cool thing about SHRDLU is that it can tell you ‘I don’t know.’ It doesn’t try to fabricate anything.”

ICM has its own ties to the modern AI world. An Anthropic employee is a member and strong supporter, and in October the museum brought six vintage computing stations to an Anthropic event, where the company’s Claude chatbot wrote programs for machines like the Commodore 64.

Back in Tukwila, Claude has become one of ICM’s most useful restoration tools. The team feeds it huge stacks of scanned documentation, such as the full set of schematics for a PDP-10. When a machine has a logic fault, they ask where to look, and Claude points them to a page in the maintenance manual or a likely bad module.

“That’s turned things that would take a couple of weeks to solve into a couple of days,” Jones said.

AI can point the way, but the repairs remain hands-on work. When Miss Piggy wouldn’t run properly this year, former Living Computers engineer Josh Dersch stepped through the machine’s microcode one instruction at a time using a recreated DEC test board, until he found a single bad chip. A spare board brought it back.

Asked if it still takes a human to keep these machines running, Jones agreed — at least for now. If a humanoid robot with AI built in ever shows up to do the repairs, he joked, “then I sit at the front desk all day.”

Nearly everything at ICM is meant to be touched, switched on and played with, and that can be intimidating. On machines with nothing but a text prompt, “you kind of have to know the commands in your head already,” Jones said.

That’s by design. From the start, the idea was to challenge people “either by confronting them with their past or demonstrating how difficult it was,” Jones said. His example: two friends, one with an Apple IIe and one with an IBM PC, whose computers “could never share” software. ICM continues that legacy, he said, “challenging the visitor to go beyond what they might feel comfortable with.”

Volunteers have written cheat sheets to help newcomers, and Jones is encouraged when families come in and kids start typing BASIC programs. “That to me was the spirit of what PDP Planet and Living Computers was meant to be,” he said.

Very little is behind glass. Jones jokes that a Timex Sinclair 1000, which would wipe your painstakingly typed program if you bumped it, is “the only computer that deserves to be behind glass.” The goal, he said, is to avoid “a boneyard that people walk through.”

Other highlights include Galaxy Game, the first coin-operated video game, installed at Stanford in 1971 and now being restored, and a Thinking Machines CM-2 supercomputer, whose 40th anniversary ICM just celebrated with members of its original design team.

For younger engineers raised on modern tools, Jones has a go-to move. He asks if they’ve used Linux, then walks them over to the PDP-7, the type of machine Unix was first written on more than 50 years ago. Many of the basic commands they type every day started there.

The collection totals about 6,000 artifacts. Volunteers are also slowly working through roughly 3,000 magnetic tapes and disk packs from Living Computers’ software preservation program. And ICM keeps 32 historic systems online, where members can log in from anywhere to use vintage operating systems, programming languages and games.

That remote access helps explain why ICM has about 500 members and sponsors, even though only 81 members have visited in person this year. Memberships start at $36 a year. Along with a King County grant through 4Culture, they help cover rent, with personal funds from Jones filling the gap. The museum hopes to become self-sustaining within five years, and maybe someday buy its own building.

The museum’s name reflects the urgency. “We’re in this magic window right now, where the hardware can still run, we can still maintain it, you can replace parts,” Jones said. “But slowly we’re getting further and further away.”

ICM is open Saturdays through Mondays from 10 a.m. to 4 p.m. Admission is $9 for adults and free for those under 18. Last May, the museum revived Vintage Computer Festival Pacific Northwest, which had been canceled in March 2020. The event drew 981 people, and it returns May 1–2 at the Tukwila Community Center.

Among the machines on the floor is MIT-AI, a DECsystem-2020 that once served as the ARPANET host for MIT’s Artificial Intelligence Lab. As Living Computers shut down in 2024, Christopher Zach, who sold the machine to Allen, told GeekWire he was ready to drive a U-Haul to Seattle to rescue it.

When a friend of Zach’s stopped by ICM’s booth at WorldCon and asked about “Chris’s computer,” Jones had a simple message to pass along: “Please just tell him it’s safe.”

Microsoft is working across Washington to help build a future-ready state where innovation creates economic opportunity and resilient communities benefit from the AI economy. From housing and transportation to education, nonprofit support and AI skills, we’re investing in the people and partnerships that help our home state thrive.

Witchbrook Has Officially Gone Gold

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The long-awaited cozy magical sim game Witchbrook has officially gone Gold, developer Chucklefish announced today. For those unfamiliar with the term “Gold," it generally means the game is complete and ready to be submitted to platforms for certification (and less likely to be delayed again).

Witchbrook is set to launch on January 28, ending a development cycle that stretches back nearly a decade. First teased in 2017, Witchbrook is a life-sim where players control a witch who begins life in the seaside town of Mossport. This includes making friends, finding love, and, most importantly, enrolling in Witchbrook College to master magic by taking classes and completing assignments.

We're delighted to announce that Witchbrook has GONE GOLD ALL PLATFORMS! 💛 We're can't for you all to join us on the journey on Jan 28th! ✨[image or embed]

Witchbrook is more or less Stardew Valley meets Hogwarts Legacy, as you can also start and run businesses, engage in seasonal events, and interact with fully fleshed-out citizens who have daily routines and schedules to consider. After a development cycle marked by long stretches of silence and several delays, those anticipating Witchbrook can breathe easier knowing the game will more than likely stick to its release date.

When Witchbrook launches on January 28, it will be available on PlayStation 5, Xbox Series X/S, Switch 2, Xbox One, Switch, and PC.
